ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u175	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the Government's draft law on the regulation of certain matters relating to the recognition of professional qualifications in connection with the appearance of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community without the conclusion of the agreement referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the Treaty on European Union, print No 3257. High Izbo! The aim of the project is to regulate the situation of citizens of the Republic of Poland and citizens of the other countries of the European Union under the conditions of an ever more pronounced prospect of disagreement between the European Union and the United Kingdom, i.e. when the so-called. An agreementless brexite. This is extremely important because one of the consequences of the unconferential brexite will be the lack of recognition in the Republic of Poland, as in the whole of the European Union, of professional qualifications acquired in the United Kingdom according to the mechanisms in force in the Member States of the European Union. These qualifications will start to be treated as qualifications acquired outside the European Union. This means that their recognition will take place on the basis of separate rules, which will force those with such qualifications to carry out much more time-consuming and costly nasal procedures. In the Republic of Poland there is no system of recognition of professional qualifications acquired in third countries. The above draft law was submitted to change this situation. March 6th this year It was addressed by the Marshal of the Sejm to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth for first reading. Four legislative amendments were tabled at the committee meeting on 13 March. The purpose of the amendments tabled was to link the entry into force of the Act with the date of the actual withdrawal of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community without the conclusion of the agreement referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the Treaty on European Union. This is due to the fact that the date of 30 March 2019 may not be the actual date on which Britain leaves the European Union. As you know very well, and this is shown especially in recent days, the situation in the British Isles is extremely dynamic and it is difficult to clearly state what the nature of the brexite will be and when it will de facto occur. Having regard to the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, 2 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the resulting principle of legal certainty should be indicated by the authority which will determine the specific date of the withdrawal of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union without the conclusion of an agreement. This authority will be the Minister responsible for the membership of the Republic of Poland in the European Union, who will publish such information by means of a notice in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland. The Commission accepted the amendments tabled. With this in mind, on behalf of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth, I would like the High Sejm to announce the draft law submitted in print no. 3275. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u176	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Teresa Wargock, Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u177	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law and the report of the committee, print No 3275. Draft law on the regulation of certain matters relating to the recognition of professional qualifications in connection with the appearance of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community without the conclusion of an agreement referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the Treaty on European Union, aims to regulate the situation of citizens of the Republic of Poland and citizens of other countries of the European Union who have acquired professional qualifications in the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland, when the so-called. An unconventional brexit. Under an increasingly prominent perspective of disagreement between the European Union and the United Kingdom on 30 March 2019, that country will become a third country in relations with the European Union and will be excluded from the Union's legal order. According to the statistics of the United Kingdom Statistical Office at the end of 2017, around 1200 000 were present in the territory of the United Kingdom. citizens of the Republic of Poland. It should be noted that of this numerous group of Polish citizens there are people who have acquired professional qualifications in the United Kingdom and if - we expect - they wish to return to Poland - they will not have to apply for their recognition in such a more extended scope when they return to Poland. Therefore, this bill protects the interests of citizens. This draft law provides for the regulation of matters such as recognition of professional qualifications for the pursuit of regulated professions and the taking up or pursuit of regulated activities. The project here provides that the decisions to recognise the qualification issued before 30 March 2019 that proceedings initiated and not completed before 30 March 2019 shall not be terminated but may continue for 42 weeks and that, thirdly, within 3 years of the United Kingdom's departure from the European Union, in principle until December 2021, Poles will be able to apply for recognition of professional qualifications acquired in the United Kingdom. These proceedings will be carried out in a simplified manner under the Qualifications Recognition Act for a maximum period of 42 months. The law also regulates the provision of cross-border services by nationals of the United Kingdom and members of their families. Since the departure of the United Kingdom from the European Union, Polish authorities will not be obliged to accept declarations of intention to provide cross-border services. The third issue is related to European Professional Cards. These registrations are no longer valid on the date of departure in the Republic of Poland. Ladies and gentlemen! The bill is needed. It protects the interests of Poles when Britain leaves the European Union without agreement. It guarantees Polish citizens the recognition of professional qualifications already acquired and serves to safeguard the interests of citizens and to exercise their right to pursue their profession. The social and economic impact of these regulations should be assessed positively. The Law and Justice Club therefore supports the proposed bill with the amendments adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

However, they will be carried out not more than 42 months from the date of entry into force of the Act. After that, they'll be decommissioned. European Professional Cards issued to citizens of the United Kingdom on entry into Poland will expire. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u182	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project under investigation is a necessity in the light of the spectrum of UK leaving the European Community. We cannot put the guilt of Poles at risk of costs and time-consuming nostrils. The Polish state is to give its citizen a helping hand. The proposed draft law provides that they will retain the power issued before 30 March 2019 to recognise professional qualifications in Poland for the pursuit of a regulated profession or for the taking up or pursuit of regulated activities acquired in the United Kingdom. They also have the power issued before 30 March 2019 to recognise in Poland the professional qualifications of citizens of the United Kingdom and members of their families acquired in one of the EU countries. The draft also provides that decisions on the recognition of the professional qualifications of the service provider issued before 30 March 2019 to which the United Kingdom citizen or a member of his family is party shall be terminated as of the date of entry into force of the Act. In accordance with the project, European Professional Cards issued to UK citizens and members of their families are valid. The government project also assumes, among other things, that citizens of Member States, including Poles, will be able to submit applications for recognition of professional qualifications acquired in the United Kingdom by 31 December 2021 at the latest. These proceedings shall be conducted under the rules laid down in the Law on the Recognition of Professional Qualifications for a period of not more than 42 months from the date of submission of the application. The exception to this regulation will be qualifications for the implementation of the so-called. sectoral professions, i.e. doctor, dentist, veterinarian, pharmacist, nurse, midwife and architect. These key assumptions are a necessity in the current situation. We as Poland should be prepared for different scenarios related to Brexit. Therefore, the PSL Club will support the proposed solutions.

The proposed law covers the activities of these institutions and implements the necessary safeguards for the interests of Polish clients who have concluded contracts with such institutions. According to the law, a foreign bank established in the United Kingdom and Northern Ireland or Gibraltar may continue to perform credit agreements until: firstly, the expiry of those contracts; secondly, the transfer of a portfolio of loans resulting from these agreements to a national bank, a branch of a foreign bank or a credit institution authorised to carry out activities consisting in granting loans in the territory of the Republic of Poland; thirdly, obtaining authorisation to carry out activities in the territory of the Republic of Poland in the form of a branch of a foreign bank; fourthly, the establishment of a national bank in the form of a public limited company - but not more than 24 months after the date of entry into force of the law. At that time, such a bank may not: conclude new credit agreements; extend the credit agreements concluded; increase the amount of cash made available to the borrower on the basis of the credit agreement concluded; make other changes to the credit agreements that increase the risk to the bank. The Act also applies to open investment funds and specialised open investment funds, as well as insurance and reinsurance activities, and transitional periods for individual activities have been consulted with the European Union. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u225	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Lidia Burzyńska, Law and Justice will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u226	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the draft parliamentary bill on the rules for determining the order of filling the seats of Members of the European Parliament elected in the Republic of Poland for the term of office of 2019 The draft provides for the adoption of a law on the rules for determining the order in which the mandates of Members of the European Parliament are to be filled. According to Art. 3 of the European Council Decision of 28 June 2018 establishing the composition of the European Parliament in the Republic of Poland will be elected 52 Members of the European Parliament. However, in case the United Kingdom continues to be a Member State of the Union at the beginning of the term of office of the European Parliament, the number of Members of the European Parliament elected in the Republic of Poland will be 51. The provisions of the Act of 5 January 2011 do not give rise to the determination and indication of which of the 52 elected Members of the European Parliament in the Republic of Poland would, in such a situation, not be entitled to a mandate at the beginning of their term of office. This would prevent all elected Members from taking over their mandates, and it is therefore necessary to regulate this matter by law. The Parliamentary draft law on the rules for determining the order in which Members of the European Parliament elected in the Republic of Poland will be filled in for the term of office of 2019 As far as the solution is concerned... It's an analogy to art. 386 § 6 of the electoral code determining the manner in which the vacant mandate is to be filled, where it cannot be covered by any of the candidates on the same list. In such a situation, the mandate shall include a candidate from another list of candidates of the same electoral committee who has received the highest number of votes in the elections, unless he has been given a mandate. By this provision, the legislator laid down a kind of order for candidates to include mandates, independent of the district list they applied for and independent of the method of allocating the mandates to the committee in question between its district lists. Thus, in this case, the State Election Commission will determine which of the Members of the European Parliament elected in elections will not include a mandate at the beginning of their term of office and will announce this information in the Official Journal of the Law. The project shall specify the mode in which these activities are to take place. The project has a positive social and legal impact. It does not entail a burden on the state budget or budgets of local government units and is not covered by European Union law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Big round of applause.]]

In the VAT Act, the project introduces regulations on the application by taxable persons of registering registers, so-called. online cash register. In addition to memory records, cash registers will provide automated, continuous and direct information to the IT system run by the head of the National Tax Administration. This system will collect and collect data from registering offices for analytical and control purposes. We estimate that the introduction of online cash registers is the first stage that my previous speaker spoke about when it comes to the introduction of the Central Register of Invoices, which is necessary in the VAT sealing system. The installation of online cash registers will result in automatic registration in the Central Repository of Cash registers without the obligation of the taxpayer to report. In the absence of communication with the central repository and inability to transmit the data on a continuous basis, the taxable person will be able to keep a full register at the register and, after obtaining the approval of the chief of the tax office, will be required to forward the data at intervals. Cash registers which are used may be used provided that they are subject to confirmation by the President of the General Office of Measurements. The project provides for a partial reimbursement of the purchase costs of the cash register and details the rules on its settlement. According to the project, the refund covers 90% purchase prices, without tax, but it is not more than 700 PLN. Using both these meters simultaneously according to the government, the online registering money will cost about 800 PLN, which is obviously not true. The percentage should either be reduced to 40% or 50% And don't fool people that it's gonna be 90%. or increase the maximum amount of refund. We must remember that buying money is not the only expense of the entrepreneur. The cost of running will include maintenance costs, data transmission costs, as well as regular technical reviews of the necessary equipment and cash register itself. Therefore, on behalf of the club, I will submit an amendment increasing the amount of the refund at a rate of up to 90% of the purchase price, but not more than PLN 1500. Here I would like to draw attention to the fact that the Minister of Finance may, by means of a regulation, exempt certain groups of taxpayers from the obligation to keep sales records using electronic or paper copies. Taxpayers may keep sales records using registers with electronic or paper copies within specified time limits. First of all, there will be the exchange of cash in the so-called. sensitive industries, and later - in others. In conclusion, we believe that the implementation of this bill fits as much as possible into the issue of sealing the tax system, and the introduction of the Central Register of Invoices by the government would have been the implementation of the statutory arrangements four years ago, i.e. 2015. Nevertheless, we believe that it is necessary to increase the support for entrepreneurs one-off - in more money than the draft law provides for. Current costs related to the operation of cash registers [[♪ The bell ♪]] On-line will remain on the side of entrepreneurs. Marshal, I'm making an adjustment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u318	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! At the moment, we are working on a draft law on the organisation of livestock breeding and breeding. This is a government initiative to implement European Union legislation, the 2016 Regulation in force from 1 November 2018. This law for the implementation of European Union legislation aims to clarify, harmonise, harmonise and collect in a single legal act certain Directives concerning the rearing of bovine animals, pigs, sheep, goats and equidae and the provisions in force in the European Union with regard to all those zootechnical arrangements concerning the manner in which those animals are kept. Of course, this implementation also applies to specific Polish conditions. In some sense, in Poland, the same rules have long been applied to the breeding of species belonging to economic animals in Poland, although to varying degrees. This applies to poultry, fur animals, bees, deer, i.e. deer, daniels, also alpaca, and mulberry silkers, which, at the request of breeders, especially those breeders, or organized ones, have also been included in livestock, although to varying degrees they benefit from the benefits of these solutions. For example, bee farmers or poultry farmers have been able for decades to raise funds for the keeping of breeding books and the evaluation of farmed animals. For example, in 2018, bee breeding was supported by grants of 1600 000 for these purposes. zł. The poultry farming was supported by a much higher amount, i.e. 7167 000. zł. The same prospects open up to other unions, to other breeders in the field of fur animals, deer, alpaca and mulberry silks. Of course, these compounds must follow the same path as the other ones, previously recorded, as regards the breeding of those species of animals which have this breeding, association tradition already very well established. But the road is the same, the conditions are identical. It is with the power of this implemented law that this applies to obvious situations such as association, or having legal personality. It is also a matter of fulfilling all the conditions that will allow breeding books to be kept, i.e. a certain organizational orement. I think that gradually all these breed groups will benefit from the benefits indicated here. Ladies and gentlemen! These are clearly amenities, solutions that are not burdened with any segment of suspicion or controversy. This was confirmed in the course of the consultation. These are obvious provisions, which are checked both on the ground of all European Union countries and in this implementation process of gradual introductions, as regards the use of these benefits also in Poland. They're transparent, they're unambiguous, they're the same for everyone, and they're not controversial. That is why we hope, with this conviction, to present to the High House these solutions, which have already repeatedly confirmed the basis for firmness and verification. Thank you kindly. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u319	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u320	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to present the position of the club Law and Justice towards the government's draft law on the organisation of livestock breeding and breeding, print no. 3175. The implementation of the arrangements envisaged in the project is due to the implementation of Regulation 2016/2012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2016 on zootechnical and genealogical conditions for the breeding, marketing and marketing of breeding animals, pure-bred breeding animals and hybrid breeding pigs within the Union and the trade in and introduction into the Union of their biological material used for breeding. The entry into force of this Regulation resulted in the repeal of most of the existing European Union legislation on zootechnics, which was implemented in the current Act of 29 June 2007 on the organisation of livestock farming and breeding. In order to eliminate the conflict between the regulation and our law, it has become necessary to bring our livestock law into line with European Union law. That Regulation regulates the following: the recognition of breeders' organisations and other entities for keeping breeding books and records, the structure and manner of keeping breeding books and records, the rules for carrying out performance and genetic evaluation, the breeding of animals and official control, but - note - only in respect of species such as cattle, pigs, sheep, goats and equidae. Our previous law of 29 June 2007 on the organisation of livestock farming and breeding also applies to other livestock species, such as poultry, fur animals, bees and deer. Therefore, the proposed bill ensures the correct application of the provisions of Regulation 2016/1012 of the European Union and, on the other hand, complements them with regulations concerning other animal species. In addition, in line with the demands of alpaca breeders, it is proposed to supplement the list of livestock with alpaca in order to allow the breeding of this species to be carried out in a manner so far appropriate for livestock. The most important solutions in the proposed law are the elimination from the law of those provisions which have been regulated by the said Regulation, because they are applied directly, and the identification of bodies and institutions to which certain powers and powers will be conferred on the Republic of Poland in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Here the minister responsible for agriculture should be mentioned - he will have the competence to: recognise breeders' organisations or other entities who apply for breeding books and registers for cattle, pigs, sheep, goats and equidae, to be breed societies and breeding enterprises, to approve breeding programmes and to impose and revoke penalties in the event of infringements - and the National Animal Breeding Centre, which will carry out official controls. Further changes are the introduction of rules, as currently in force, to ensure the continuity of livestock farming of species such as poultry, fur animals, bees and deer. This is complemented, as I mentioned, by alpacas not covered by the EU Regulation. These include rules on rules for keeping breeding books and records, performance and genetic evaluation and requirements for breeding animals. In addition, as regards the amendment of these provisions, material provisions are introduced which provide the basis for State aid for the keeping of breeding books and the assessment of the performance and genetic value of livestock. For these reasons, given the need to bring our livestock legislation into line with European Union legislation and to supplement it with the rules on animal species not covered by Regulation 2016/1012, we consider the proposal to be coherent, comprehensive and transparent. The Law and Justice Club will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u321	Thank you very much. Mrs Iwona Krawczyk, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u332	The main objective of the project is to adapt Polish regulations to the current EU legislation on breeding and breeding of bovine animals, pigs, sheep, goats and equidae. The aim of the project is to implement the provisions of Regulation (EU) 2016/1012 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2016 on zootechnical and genealogical conditions for the breeding, marketing and marketing of pure-bred breeding animals and hybrid breeding pigs within the Union and trade in and introduction into the Union of their biological material for breeding and introduction into the Union and amending Regulation (EU) No 652/2014, Council Directives 89/608/EEC and 90/ 425/EEC and repealing certain acts in the field of animal husbandry. National rules on breeding and breeding of non-Union animals, namely cervids, poultry, fur animals and honey bees, have been laid down. In addition, the range of these species has been extended to include alpaca and mulberry silk. As for these regulations, I have two comments. Firstly, alpacas as farm animals are not yet covered by the relevant guidelines on the conditions of their maintenance and farming and their well-being. In 2016, it was estimated that the alpaca in Poland was around 3,000. pieces, in 2018 approx. 4,000. It is difficult to establish their current number as they have so far been associated animals and no one has kept a breeding register of these animals. When, therefore, will the minimum requirements for keeping alpaca in the context of the proposed changes give alpacas the status of breeding animals be defined? These appropriate animal conditions must be taken care of. With regard to animal welfare, my second point in this matter concerns the breeding of fur animals. Unfortunately, the announcements of the PiS concerning the ban on animal breeding for fur were merely a soap of the eyes of voters. Most European Union countries have already introduced this ban, so fur animal breeders benefit from the opportunities that Poland allows in its law. The Ministry of Agriculture declared the tightening of the farm control criteria in the past year, but at the moment it is difficult to verify these promises. According to the latest report of the European Court of Auditors on livestock There are still irregularities. Although the European Commission has issued guidelines on the transport conditions and methods of slaughter of animals and the welfare of pigs, there are still extreme deviations in their practical application, as demonstrated by the latest journalistic case of animal treatment in slaughterhouses and trade in bovine meat from sick cows. It is also a problem for some Member States to delay the implementation of recommendations in national law. Although this project does not concern animal welfare, the regulations on farming imply the need to regulate the living conditions of animals in breeding, in this case new animals on the livestock list in Poland - alpaca and mulberry. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-14-sejm-78-2.u341	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I would like to present today the figure of a great character, Prof. Dr. Hab. Bolesław Fleszar, who received the honorary doctorate of the Rzeszów University of Technology. This distinction was granted by the Senate of the University in recognition of Professor Fleszar's achievements in the field of physical, analytical, electrochemistry and his merits in the transfer of historical truth and the formation of democratic attitudes, as well as in recognition of his invaluable contribution to scientific development and patriotic formation of many generations of students. The professor's most important scientific achievement was the development of a new model of double electric layer, so-called. a polarisation model. The professor promoted five doctors, two of whom are independent scientists, developing electrochemical methods in their research. They promoted a total of eight doctors. It can therefore be concluded that the professor is the creator of the Rzeszow scientific electrochemical school. Prof. Fleszar's scientific achievements include several dozen original works published in scientific journals, 13 patents, dozens of commissioned research studies and numerous conferences. In recognition of the importance of this acquis in 1996. The Central Qualifications Commission granted him the title of professor of chemical sciences. The professor's contribution to the educational and organizational development of the Rzeszow University of Technology is invaluable. Prof. Bolesław Fleszar organized a system of chemistry teaching for non-chemical students. He was one of the main organizers of the Faculty of Chemical Technology, which inaugurated his activities in 1968. In 1973 Prof. Bolesław Fleszar for political reasons, for the content of the lecture inaugurating the academic year was removed from performing administrative functions at the university. In September 1980, he joined the "Solidarity" and was soon elected to the bureau of the work commission of this association at his home university. He also carried out union activities outside the polytechnics. He's assigned to him, for example. authored by many speeches during meetings and solidarity demonstrations in 1987 and 1989. The undisputed authority of the professor caused him to be elected as the rector of the Rzeszow University of Technology in the first democratic elections of the university authorities in Poland in 1981. Unfortunately, less than a year later, during a period of martial law, he was dismissed from his post. Since 1981 Prof. Bolesław Fleszar has co-edited an independent cultural and social monthly magazine called Reduta. Between 1985 and 1990, he was a lecturer in the academic, working and agricultural diocese of Przemysl and a member of a team of independent historians. In 1989 Prof. Fleszar became a member of the Civic Committee and on the list of this committee was elected Senator of the Republic of Poland for the first term of office. In these elections he gained almost 80% important votes. In the Senate committees he dealt with matters of education, science, culture and the media. Among other important projects of Professor Fleszar I would like to mention that the professor with the help of other activists led to the construction of the monument of General Władysław Sikorski in Rzeszów. 30 years ago, in such a difficult time for Polish democracy, he also participated in the work of the Committee of the Obstetrics of the 70th anniversary of the Independence of Poland. I am glad that just now, 30 years later, on the 100th anniversary of those events Professor Bolesław Fleszar was honoured with the honorary title of Doctor of Honoris causa Rzeszow University of Technology. Prof. Bolesław Fleszar has been awarded many times for his activities, among others. Medal of the Committee on National Education, the Order of Rebirth of Poland by the Bachelor's Cross, and in 1999 Prof. Bolesław Fleszar received the scientific award of the town of Rzeszów. The professor's scientific achievements represent an immeasurable value for subsequent generations, which, when entering into professional life, have the opportunity to benefit from his knowledge and achievements on the basis of science. The title awarded to Dr. Honoris causa is the culmination of Professor Bolesław Fleszar's many years of scientific and social work. Currently waiting for the removal from the hands of the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda of the Order of the White Eagle. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
